      116  - `SubFileSystem` - confines access to a sub-path of another `
           IFileSystem`, resolved purely lexically (no real disk access, 
           unlike `PhysicalFileSystem`): no path as written can leave its
            base, but a symbolic link under its base on a physical filesy
           stem leads wherever that filesystem lets it
      117  - `ComposedFileSystem` - overlays one `IFileSystem` inside ano
           ther at a path, without touching either; it is a filesystem th
           at delegates to `main` with the overlay registered as a mount 
           point
      118  - `MountableFileSystem` - the base every filesystem here deriv
           es from; implements `Mount`/`Unmount` and the routing they nee
           d, so a subclass only implements the `Local*` operations for t
           he paths it owns itself
      104 -- `MountPoints` - the mount table behind that: longest-prefix 
          -path matching, plus the file-descriptor translation a mount re
          -quires (the two filesystems hand out descriptors from independ
          -ent namespaces that both start at 3, so a mounted file's descr
          -iptor is re-issued from a range far above any real one and can
          - never be confused with the host's). The synthetic range is al
          -located from one program-wide counter (`AllocateSyntheticFd`),
          - not one per table: filesystems stack, and an inner one's synt
          -hetic fd passes up through the outer one untranslated, so per-
          -table counters would collide. Builtin command files take their
          - fds from the same counter.

● fd--process-table is merged into develop as 0.4.2 (PR #20, squash commit
  42cec68).

  - Fixed by the reviewer (high): a race in one test.
    OpenFileHandsBackAnUnnumberedDescriptor used a Lua script that ends within
    milliseconds and releases its descriptors. That could occasionally leave a
    slot empty just before the test wrote through it, causing a crash. The test
    now waits for the script to finish first.
  - Security: no malice found. The 4 files the gate marked for review were plain
    documentation edits and a CMake test entry.
  - Left open:
    - Medium: the same fake test class, ReleaseCountingDescriptor, now exists in
      three test files. It belongs in one header under tests/mocks/.
    - Low: a misleading comment in Cat.cpp:217.
  - Cost: 248,684 Claude tokens (plan check, task and review agents), plus
    117,273 input and 30,383 output tokens on Ollama.

  The playbook, HAISOS_VERSION, the review record and the develop PR #18 are
  updated.

  Next is streams--console-and-start. Its plan was written before both fd tasks
  and names several files they changed, so a helper agent is re-checking it
  against the current code. I've asked it to fold in this review's two lessons
  where the plan's tests already need them:
  - use one shared fake in tests/mocks/ rather than a fourth copy;
  - don't let tests race a process that exits at once.

  | critical | fixed in 89a1a2b | src/components/HaisosOS/LuaProcess.cpp:494 |
  RenderLuaError called an error object's __tostring through luaL_callmeta after
  lua_pcall had returned, so outside any protection. If that __tostring raises,
  or a kill lands while it runs, Lua panics and calls abort(), which kills the
  whole haisos host. Any untrusted .lua script could do this. Fix: the error is
  now rendered by a message handler inside the protected call, as lua.c does,
  with the new test LuaProcessTest.AnErrorObjectsTostringRunsProtected. Looks
  like an oversight in following the plan, not malice. |
  | medium | open | src/components/HaisosOS/LuaProcess.cpp:614 | exit() inside a
  coroutine re-arms the hook only on the coroutine's thread. coroutine.resume
  catches the error, so the main thread can run up to about 1000 more
  instructions, tool calls included, before its own count hook fires. |
  | medium | open | src/components/Agent/Agent.cpp:507 | No test covers a

  of <n> LLM rounds" error line). |

  checks "finished?" and then sets the stop flag. An agent that finishes on its

  input loop still waits for a line is also counted as stopped when the OS shuts
  down. |

      180  
      181 +## Exit codes                                                 
      182 +                                                              
      183 +Every process reports an exit code, `IProcess::ExitCode()`: em
          +pty while it                                                  
      184 +runs, then a shell-style 0-255 that never changes once set. A 
          +program's own                                                 
      185 +code is taken modulo 256 (`exit(256)` is 0, `exit(-1)` is 255)
          +; a process                                                   
      186 +stopped through `TriggerStop()` reports 143 (128 + SIGTERM, as
          + a shell would                                                
      187 +for a killed program); 141 (128 + SIGPIPE) is reserved for the
          + pipes work to                                                
      188 +come. A `RUN` whose process never starts has no code -- as wit
          +h a shell's 127,                                              
      189 +reporting it is the launcher's business, not the process's.   
      190 +                                                              
      191 +haisos's own exit status is the exit code of the first `RUN` (
          +in file order)                                                
      192 +that did not exit 0 -- 127 when one or no process could be sta
          +rted, else 0 --                                               
      193 +with haisos's own errors (a bad haisosfile, an unreadable `OUT
          +COPY` target)                                                 
      194 +exiting 1. What a runtime counts as its code: a builtin, the c
          +ommand's own                                                  
      195 +status; a Lua script, `exit([code])`'s argument (0 by default,
          + `true` 0 and                                                 
      196 +`false` 1, as `os.exit` -- the stock `os.exit` stays unopened)
          +, or 1 on a load                                              
      197 +or runtime error, which also writes `lua: <message>` to the sc
          +ript's stderr                                                 
      198 +just as the standalone interpreter does; an agent, 1 when its 
          +last command                                                  
      199 +failed (an LLM, HTTP or parse failure, the LLM-round cap, an e
          +xception), else                                               
      200 +0. Each runtime decides in one place, `ExitCodeFor` in        
      201 +`src/components/libheaders/ExitCodes.h`.                      
      202 +                                                              
      203  ## The `haisosfile` DSL
      204  
      205  A small Dockerfile-style language (parsed by `HaisosFileParser
           ` in `src/haisos/`;

  ⎿  Backgrounded agent (↓ to manage · ctrl+o to expand)

● streams--exit-codes is merged into develop as 0.4.5 (PR #23, squash commit
  2cb3d48).

  - Fixed by the reviewer (critical): any .lua script could crash the whole
    haisos host. A script could raise an error whose printable form itself
    fails, and that form was being computed outside Lua's error protection. Lua
    then panicked and aborted the process. The error is now rendered inside the
    protected call, as the standard Lua interpreter does, and a new test covers
    it. The reviewer judged it an oversight, not malice.
  - Left open:
    - Medium: calling exit() inside a Lua coroutine doesn't stop the script at
      once. The script can run up to about 1,000 more instructions, tool calls
      included (LuaProcess.cpp:614).
    - Medium: no test covers an agent command failing when it hits the LLM-round
      limit (Agent.cpp:507).
    - Low: 3 findings: a small timing race in TriggerStop, a loose cast in
      exit(), and unclear exit-status docs.
  - Cost: 334,302 Claude tokens, plus 228,366 input and 65,962 output tokens on
    Ollama.

  Decision for you: should the two medium findings get a small follow-up task
  now, or wait for the final-review fixes? I've added this to the playbook's
  Questions. Until you answer it, they wait for the final review, and the
  develop keeps going in the meantime. To answer it, use /develop-plan begin in
  the plan session and publish with /develop-update.

  Next is pipes--pipe-service, the first task of the pipes rock. Its plan names
  13 files that have changed since it was written, so a helper agent is
  re-checking it against the current code. I've also asked that its tests give
  blocking pipe reads and writes a timeout, so a bug can't hang the test run.

● pipes--pipe-service is merged into develop as 0.4.6 (PR #24, squash commit
  4e48555). I merged it myself once the Windows fix passed all three CI checks.

  - Windows fix: the helper agent changed one test file (18 lines added, 8
    removed). The HaisosOS.unittests test program now takes about 29.5 s on
    Windows, down from 31.9 s, so it fits CI's 30 s limit. Most of the time went
    to a fake LLM address, 127.0.0.1:9999, which Windows takes about 2 seconds
    to refuse. The new tests now use 0.0.0.0:9999, which fails at once, and wait
    50 ms instead of 200 and 500 ms.
  - Left open:
    - Medium: CreatePipe takes its lock once per pipe end, which risks a race
      with Dup2.
    - Low: a garbled comment.
    - Low: test assertions that could crash PipeServiceTest instead of failing
      cleanly.
  - Cost: 499,370 Claude tokens, the Windows fix included, plus 157,878 input
    and 54,555 output tokens on Ollama. That doesn't count the review cut off by
    the session limit, whose usage wasn't reported.

  New question for you (in the playbook): HaisosOS.unittests still runs at about
  29.5 s on Windows against the 30 s limit. The existing HaisosOSTest.cpp alone
  takes about 29.4 s, because it still uses the slow address. Any later task
  that adds HaisosOS tests is likely to turn Windows red. There are three ways
  out:
  - Raise the timeout. That's a change to scripts/ or .github/, so it has to
    come from the plan session or master.
  - Add a small task to switch HaisosOSTest to 0.0.0.0:9999.
  - Split the test program.

  Until you answer, tasks go on, and any Windows timeout gets fixed on the host
  as it was here. Your earlier question about the two medium findings from #23
  is also still open. Answer both in the plan session with /develop-plan begin,
  then /develop-update.

  was written, so a helper agent is re-checking it. I've asked it to also apply
  what the recent reviews taught, where the plan's tests need it, including the
  Windows timing points.

  ⎿  Updated CLAUDE.md (+3 -2)
      1  # Haisos - C++ Platform for Running Agents
      2  
      3 -Haisos is a C++ platform that boots a small OS-like environment 
        -(`IHaisosOS`) from a `haisosfile` manifest and runs processes in
        - it: LLM agents (`.md` files, sent to a local LLM like Ollama wi
        -th tool-calling), embedded Lua scripts (`.lua` files), and built
        -in commands (`echo`, `cat`, `ls`, ... compiled into Haisos and p
        -laced on a filesystem at a path, such as `/bin/ls`).            
      3 +Haisos is a C++ platform that boots a small OS-like environment 
        +(`IHaisosOS`) from a `haisosfile` manifest and runs processes in
        + it: LLM agents (`.md` files, sent to a local LLM like Ollama wi
        +th tool-calling), embedded Lua scripts (`.lua` files), and built
        +in commands (`echo`, `cat`, `ls`, `wc`, ... compiled into Haisos
        + and placed on a filesystem at a path, such as `/bin/ls`).      
      4  
      5  ## Project Overview
      6  
     ...
      28  ├── src/
      29  │   ├── components/        - Component implementations (each ha
          s its own CLAUDE.md)
      30  │   │   ├── Agent/

         - ls, mkdir, pwd; each in commands/<name>/) and what places them

● builtins--wc is merged into develop as 0.4.10 (PR #28, squash commit 23e7ccb).
  That makes 10 tasks done.

  - Fixed by the reviewer (high): nothing tested a multibyte character split
    across wc's 16 KiB read chunks. The reviewer added
    WcCharacterSplitAcrossReadChunksCountsOnce, and CI passed on Linux and
    Windows before the merge.
  - Left open (medium): with an empty --files0-from list, --total=always and
    --total=only print nothing. GNU wc still prints the total line, so this
    breaks the rule that builtins print what the real command prints. I've noted
    it for the final review's fixes.
  - Left open (low): 4 findings:
    - a read error on - is named "standard input";
    - an extra string copy per chunk;
    - some indentation;
    - a long comment line.
  - Security: no findings. Wc.cpp reaches files, stdin and the environment only
    through the current process, as the rules require.
  - Cost: 284,222 Claude tokens, plus 137,113 input and 57,447 output tokens on
    Ollama. The two review attempts killed by the API's 529 "Overloaded" error
    reported no usage.

  Next is builtins--man. Its plan names 7 files that have changed since it was
  written, so a helper agent is re-checking it against the current code.

  Your two questions are still open:
  - a follow-up task for the Lua coroutine gap;
  - how to handle the Windows 30 s limit.

  ⎿  Created src/components/BuiltinCommands/commands/hsh/CLAUDE.md (+72 -0)
       1 +# hsh                                                          
       2 +                                                               
       3 +hsh is the Haisos SHell: a reimplementation of POSIX `sh` model
         +led on dash                                                    
       4 +(Debian/Ubuntu's `/bin/sh`), to become a builtin command. All o
         +f it lives in                                                  
       5 +namespace `Haisos::Hsh`, in this directory, as plain portable C
         +++17 (it is                                                    
       6 +built for Linux, Windows/MSVC and WASM: no POSIX headers, no `<
         +regex>`).                                                      
       7 +                                                               
       8 +The pipeline is lexer -> parser -> expansion -> executor; each 
         +stage is a                                                     
       9 +task of the hsh rock and adds its files here:                  
      10 +                                                               
      11 +- `HshError.h` - `ShellError`, the one error type every stage t
         +hrows: the                                                     
      12 +  message is what dash prints after `hsh: <line>: ` (see       
      13 +  `FormatShellError`), and `Incomplete()` tells an interactive 
         +shell that the                                                 
      14 +  error happened only because the input ended where more was ne
         +eded -- it                                                     
      15 +  shows PS2, reads another line and tries again.               
      16 +- `HshWord.h/.cpp` - the word model: a `Word` is a tree of `Wor
         +dPart`s that                                                   
      17 +  keeps its quoting: `Literal` (unquoted text), `Quoted` (text 
         +that was                                                       
      18 +  quoted: `'...'`, `\c`, the plain text inside `"..."` or a her
         +edoc body),                                                    
      19 +  `DoubleQuoted` (a `"..."` group, its contents in `parts`), `P
         +arameter`                                                      
      20 +  (`$name` / `${...}`, with `op` and operand `parts`), `Command
         +Substitution`                                                  
      21 +  (`$(...)` or `` `...` ``, the source in `text`) and `Arithmet
         +ic`                                                            
      22 +  (`$((...))`). Adjacent Literal parts are merged into one, and
         + so are                                                        
      23 +  adjacent Quoted parts, at every level. `LiteralText(word)` gi
         +ves the word's                                                 
      24 +  text when it is unquoted literal only -- how reserved words, 
         +for-loop                                                       
      25 +  variables and assignment prefixes are recognized. Also `HereD
         +ocument`, the                                                  
      26 +  record a `<<`/`<<-` redirection fills in.                    
      27 +- `HshLexer.h/.cpp` - the `Lexer`: reads a source string token 
         +by token (words                                                
      28 +  with their parts, operators, IO numbers, newlines, end of inp
         +ut), counts                                                    
      29 +  lines, removes `\`-newline continuations everywhere but singl
         +e quotes,                                                      
      30 +  comments and quoted-delimiter heredoc bodies, and attaches a 
         +`HereDocument`                                                 
      31 +  to the word after `<<`/`<<-`, its body read at the newline th
         +at ends the                                                    
      32 +  line (all pending heredocs, in order). Each token carries `be
         +gin`/`end`                                                     
      33 +  byte offsets into the source so the parser can cut `ListItem:
         +:sourceText`                                                   
      34 +  and `FunctionDefinition::sourceText` out of it. `AsReservedWo
         +rd` spells out                                                 
      35 +  which reserved word a word token spells; whether it IS one is
         + the parser's                                                  
      36 +  business.                                                    
      37 +- (later tasks: the parser/AST, expansion, the executor that re
         +gisters the                                                    
      38 +  `hsh` builtin, the interactive loop.)                        
      39 +                                                               
      40 +## How the end of a `$(...)` is found                          
     … 32 more lines (ctrl+o to expand)

● Agent(Re-check hsh--parser plan) Sonnet 5
  ⎿  Backgrounded agent (↓ to manage · ctrl+o to expand)

● hsh--lexer is merged into develop as 0.4.12 (PR #30, squash commit 3ccc87c).
  That's 12 tasks done, with 9 hsh tasks left.

  - Fixed by the reviewer (high):
    - ${#:-x} was rejected; it now reads as $# with :-, as in dash.
    - In an unquoted heredoc body, a ' inside ${...} threw "Unterminated quoted
      string", where dash prints it. Such operands are now read as if inside
      double quotes.

    Both came with tests, and CI passed on Linux and Windows.
  - Left open (medium): three places where the lexer differs from dash.
    - A lexing error inside a heredoc body still asks for more input, so an
      interactive shell would keep showing its continuation prompt.
    - A ' inside a ${x#...} pattern within double quotes isn't treated as
      quoting.
    - A \ at the end of a line before an unquoted heredoc delimiter doesn't join
      the lines.

    The goal says hsh copies dash, so these count as fixes toward the plan, not
    new decisions. Each goes into the later hsh task that touches that code: the
    parser, expansion or interactive plan.
  - Left open (low): Lexer can't be moved, and it has an unused constructor.
    Both go into the parser task too.
  - Cost: 280,991 Claude tokens, plus 121,098 input and 75,276 output tokens on
    Ollama.

  Next is hsh--parser. A helper agent is re-checking its plan against the
  lexer's actual API, and adding two fixes as first steps:
  - make Lexer movable;

● hsh--parser is merged into develop as 0.4.13 (PR #31, squash commit d26cf22).
  That's 13 tasks done, with 8 hsh tasks left.

  - Fixed by the reviewer (high):
    - The source text kept for commands inside { ... }, a case branch, or a
      function followed by more on its line was cut off before the heredoc body
      was read, so the body was lost. The parser's round-trip tests compare AST
      dumps, which never show heredoc bodies, so they couldn't catch it.
      Exact-text tests now do.
    - An error inside a closed $(...) or backquote was treated as "needs more
      input", so an interactive shell would hang after echo $(if).

    Both have tests, and CI passed on Linux and Windows.
  - Left open (medium):
    - An escaped \\ before a newline in a heredoc still joins the line, unlike
      dash. It should join only on an odd number of trailing backslashes.
    - The AST dump format is documented nowhere, though comments in HshAst.h
      point to sections that don't exist.
  - Left open (low): 3 findings: two dash details to verify, and an extra source
    copy.
  - Cost: 294,357 Claude tokens, plus 158,416 input and 94,423 output tokens on
    Ollama.

  I'll fold the two medium findings into the hsh--expansion plan, the next task
  that touches that code. Its re-check will also take #30's pattern-quoting fix.

  Next is hsh--arith-glob. Its plan names 6 files that have changed, including
  the file interfaces the fd tasks reworked. A helper agent is re-checking it
  against the current code. If any arithmetic or glob rule in the plan differs
  from dash, the agent will report it rather than change the plan on its own.

  Your two questions are still open:
  - a follow-up task for the Lua coroutine gap;
  - how to handle the Windows 30 s limit.

› Message from a472bd41eb344f7dd
  VERDICT: merged
  PR: #32  MERGED AS: d60a719  REVIEWED HEAD:
  06ab0e74743311ca709c2c06cdfe16063987a45c  WINDOWS: green
  FINDINGS:
  | critical | fixed in 06ab0e7 |
  src/components/BuiltinCommands/commands/hsh/HshArithmetic.cpp:84 | Tokenize
  hung on Windows. On "0x" with no hex digit after it, MSVC's strtoimax reads
  nothing (end == start), so the index never moved and tokens piled up without
  end. The " 0x " test row triggered it: this is why Hsh.unittests was killed
  after 40 s. Fix: when nothing is read, take the "0" as glibc does. Added "0x"
  and "0xg" test rows. After the fix every check passed, Windows included. |
  | medium | open | HshArithmetic.cpp:184 (ParseAssign) | The variable on the
  left is read before the right-hand side, and also for plain `=`. dash reads it
  only for compound operators, after the right-hand side. So `x=abc; $((x=3))`
  fails here with "Illegal number: abc", and `x=5; $((x += (x=2)))` gives 7
  where dash gives 4. |
  | medium | open | HshGlob.cpp:14 (SplitComponents) | It jumps over a bracket
  expression even when the bracket holds a '/', so `[a/b]` stays one component
  and matches the file `a`. The plan, like dash, says a bracket never spans a
  '/'. |
  | low | open | HshPattern.h:39 | PatternBracketEnd is public API that the plan
  does not list. |
  | low | open | HshPattern.cpp:16 (BracketEnd) | A `[:` searches for `:]`
  anywhere further on, so it can run past the bracket's real closing `]`. |
  Security: no concerns. The gate's REVIEW items are harmless: the two
  CMakeLists.txt files only add sources, and the CLAUDE.md lines only describe
  the new files. There is no file, process or network access, and globbing reads
  only through IPathnameSource.
  FOLLOW-UP: HshArithmetic.cpp ParseAssign: for plain `=` do not read the
  variable; for compound operators read it after evaluating the right-hand side
  (dash's lookup order).
  HshGlob.cpp SplitComponents: jump over a bracket only when no '/' lies inside

● hsh--arith-glob is merged into develop as 0.4.14 (PR #32, squash commit
  d60a719). That's 14 tasks done, with 7 left.

  - Fixed by the reviewer (critical): the hang that killed Hsh.unittests on
    Windows. On 0x with no hex digit after it, MSVC's strtoimax reads nothing,
    so the tokenizer never advanced and looped forever; glibc reads the 0. It
    now takes the 0 the way glibc does. Two new test rows cover it, and all
    checks passed afterwards, Windows included.
  - Left open (medium): two places where hsh differs from dash.
    - Arithmetic assignment reads the variable too early. x=abc; $((x=3)) fails
      with "Illegal number", and x=5; $((x += (x=2))) gives 7 where dash
      gives 4.
    - A glob bracket can span a /, so [a/b] matches a file named a.
  - Left open (low): two small pattern-matching issues.
  - Cost: 247,262 Claude tokens, plus 93,336 input and 49,955 output tokens on
    Ollama.

  real lexer, parser, arithmetic and glob code. It is also adding five dash

  ⎿  Created develop-plan/tasks/streams--coroutine-latch.md (+165 -0)
        1 +# Task streams--coroutine-latch: Lua exit/broken pipe stop cor
          +outines' resumers at once; round-cap test                     
        2 +                                                              
        3 +- Rock: streams                                               
        4 +- Depends on: streams--exit-codes, pipes--broken-pipe         
        5 +- Size: ~200 changed lines in ~4 files                        
        6 +- Plan checked against: develop @ 7e857e9                     
        7 +- PR title: Lua: latch exit and broken pipe across coroutines;
          + test the LLM round cap                                       
        8 +                                                              
        9 +## Goal                                                       
       10 +                                                              
       11 +A Lua script that calls `exit(n)`, or whose `print` hits a pip
          +e nobody reads,                                               
       12 +from inside a coroutine stops **before the next instruction** 
          +of the thread                                                 
       13 +that resumed the coroutine, exactly as it does outside one. It
          + never runs                                                   
       14 +another instruction or another tool call. It exits `n`, or 141
          + for the                                                      
       15 +broken pipe. Today the latched kill hook is re-armed only on t
          +he coroutine's                                                
       16 +own thread. `coroutine.resume` catches the "exit" error and re
          +turns `false`,                                                
       17 +and the resuming thread runs on until its next count hook, up 
          +to ~1000                                                      
       18 +instructions, tool calls included.                            
       19 +                                                              
       20 +The agent's LLM round cap (20 rounds per command) also gets th
          +e test it lacks.                                              
       21 +                                                              
       22 +## Context                                                    
       23 +                                                              
       24 +Read first: the root `CLAUDE.md` (Security: `ICurrentProcess`)
          +, and                                                         
       25 +`src/components/HaisosOS/CLAUDE.md`.                          
       26 +                                                              
       27 +- `src/components/HaisosOS/LuaProcess.cpp`:                   
       28 +  - `KillHookTrampoline` (~line 256): the latched hook. When  
       29 +    `IsKillRequested() || IsExitRequested()` it re-arms itself
          + on `L` with                                                  
       30 +    `LUA_MASKCOUNT | LUA_MASKCALL | LUA_MASKRET | LUA_MASKLINE
          +, 1` and raises                                               
       31 +    `luaL_error(L, "process killed")`. `StopForBrokenPipe()` s
          +ets                                                           
       32 +    `m_brokenPipe` and calls `Kill()`, so a broken pipe counts
          + as a kill.                                                   
       33 +  - `LuaPrintTrampoline` (~line 593): on `m_brokenPipe` it re-
          +arms the hook on                                              
       34 +    `L` only. It must never raise, because it has C++ locals. 
       35 +  - `LuaExitTrampoline` (~line 631): sets the exit flags, re-a
          +rms the hook on                                               
       36 +    `L` only, and raises `luaL_error(L, "exit")`.             
       37 +  - `m_luaState` is the main thread. `OpenSafeLuaLibs` (~line 
          +212) opens                                                    
       38 +    `coroutine`. `lua_sethook` acts on one `lua_State` (thread
          +) only. A                                                     
       39 +    coroutine is a separate thread. It inherits the hook setti
          +ngs of the                                                    
       40 +    thread that creates it, but later `lua_sethook` calls on o
          +ne thread do                                                  
     … 125 more lines (ctrl+o to expand)

● streams--coroutine-latch is merged into develop as 0.4.15 (PR #33, squash
  commit b98ff82). This closes the Lua coroutine gap you asked about. That's 15
  tasks done, with 7 hsh tasks left.

  - Fixed by the reviewer:
    - Critical: the new coroutine.wrap wrapper ignored how many arguments it
      got. So coroutine.wrap(f, x) called f(x) straight away instead of making a
      coroutine, and coroutine.wrap() broke the Lua API's rules. Any script
      could trigger that.
    - High: errors from wrapped coroutines lost their file:line: prefix.
    - High: the new round-cap test was flaky; it read the error line before the
      agent had written it.
    - Medium: bad-argument errors named the function '?'.

    Each fix has a test, and all CI checks passed, Windows included.
  - Left open:
    - Medium: a negligible difference in how memory errors are prefixed, noted
      in a code comment.
    - Low: one over-long line in CLAUDE.md.
  - Cost: 167,180 Claude tokens, plus 76,660 input and 17,139 output tokens on
    Ollama.

  Next is hsh--expansion. Its plan re-check is running again: it updates the
  plan to the real hsh APIs and adds the five dash fixes from #30–#32 as first
  steps. It will also say whether the plan now runs past about 1,100 lines, and
  if so which fixes to move out.

● hsh--expansion is merged into develop as 0.4.16 (PR #34, squash commit
  ca0002b). That's 16 tasks done, with 6 hsh tasks left.

  - Fixed by the reviewer (high): it checked each disputed case against a real
    dash 0.5.12.
    - Inside double quotes, the operand of #, ##, % and %% was taken literally.
      So "${x%/*}" removed a literal /*, where dash treats it as a pattern.
    - Kimi-k3's tilde rules for ${...} operands went against dash, and its tests
      asserted the wrong results. a${z:=~/a} and ${q:+~} now expand the ~, as
      dash does. The tests and the hsh CLAUDE.md are corrected.
  - Windows: the HaisosOS timeout didn't come back. CI passed on the reviewer's
    new head, Windows included.
  - Left open (medium): "$@$@" with no arguments gives no field, where dash
    gives one empty field. Only a lone "$@" should vanish.
  - Cost: 329,469 Claude tokens, plus 528,163 input and 111,701 output tokens on
    Ollama.

  Next is hsh--executor, which runs parsed commands through
  IHaisosOS::StartProcess. Its plan was written before most of this develop and
  names 13 files that have changed since. A helper agent is re-checking it
  against the current headers and adding the "$@$@" fix as a first step.

  The plan is already about 1,000 lines, and executor tests start processes. So
  I've asked the agent for two things:
  - a new size estimate;
  - a count of any new HaisosOS tests, keeping new tests out of
    HaisosOS.unittests where possible, because of the Windows time limit.

  change): `Haisos::Hsh::Expander::WalkDoubleQuoted` in
  src/components/BuiltinCommands/commands/hsh/HshExpansion.cpp (~line 290) used

  `Fields({""})`. Built release Linux and ran `test_linux.sh L U` (21/21 suites

  (`WaitToFinish`) before reading `ExitCode()`/output in every case

● hsh--redirections is merged into develop as 0.4.18 (PR #36, squash commit
  6c7185e). That's 18 tasks done, with 4 left: pipelines, shell builtins,
  control flow and interactive.

  - Fixed by the reviewer (high): the new heredoc line-joining code could read
    past the end of an empty string, which is undefined behaviour. That happened
    with <<- when the last body line was only tabs and had no newline; the old
    size check had been dropped. It now has a guard and a lexer test, and CI
    passed on Linux and Windows.
  - Left open (low): 5 findings:
    - a redirection descriptor like x=' 3' or x=+3 is accepted where dash wants
      exactly one digit;
    - two edge cases in saving and restoring descriptors, one with a full
      descriptor table and one when a second redirection attempt fails;
    - a glob class that can run past a /;
    - a CLAUDE.md typo.

    I'll fold the two most relevant into the hsh--control-flow re-check: the
    single-digit rule, and save/restore across repeated attempts.
  - Cost: 311,235 Claude tokens, plus 129,265 input and 49,476 output tokens on
    Ollama.

  Next is hsh--pipelines. Its plan is already at about 1,000 lines. A helper
  agent is re-checking it against the current pipe, process and redirection
  headers. It is also adding a first step that fixes #35's racy
  StopEndsTheShellAndItsChild test. It's told to edit only the plan file and to
  say if the plan goes past about 1,100 lines.

● Agent(Re-check hsh--shell-builtins plan) Sonnet 5  ⎿  Backgrounded agent (↓ to manage · ctrl+o to expand)

● hsh--pipelines is merged into develop as 0.4.19 (PR #37, squash commit
  7598ffa). That's 19 tasks done, with 3 left: shell builtins, control flow and
  interactive.

  - Fixed by the reviewer (high): a $(...) inside a pipeline or background stage
    inherited "don't wait". Its commands ran at the same time as the stage, $?
    inside was always 0, and the child processes leaked. It's fixed with a new
    test, and CI passed, Windows included.
  - Left open (medium): 3 findings.
    - : | /bin/echo $(cat) deadlocks.
    - A background $(...) blocks the shell, as in /bin/echo $(/spin.lua) &.
    - Jobs started inside a subshell stay in the shell's list of live child
      processes for good.
  - Left open (low): 4 findings. One is a documented deviation: options reach a
    child hsh as an invocation argument, because there was no set builtin yet.
  - Cost: 297,337 Claude tokens, plus 152,300 input and 68,255 output tokens on
    Ollama.

  The three remaining plans are each already at about 800–1,000 lines. So I'm
  folding into each only the follow-ups that task really needs:
  - hsh--control-flow gets the deadlock, the subshell job leak, and the
    redirection save/restore fix from #36, since all three matter once loops
    exist.
  - The background-$(...) issue and the single-digit descriptor rule wait for
    the final review's fixes task.

  Next is hsh--shell-builtins. A helper agent is re-checking its plan against
  the shell code merged since it was written, for example wait came with
  pipelines. It is also adding three dash fixes from #35:
  - -o inside a cluster of option letters;
  - the read-only check after expanding the value;
  - a log warning for a child that ignores a stop.

  It's told to edit only the plan and to report the new size.

  83f2e46). That's 20 tasks done, with 2 left: control flow and interactive.

      fixed, the hsh CLAUDE.md is corrected, and there's a test.

      - cd reaching child processes, and PWD/OLDPWD;

  - Fixed by the reviewer (low): a missing #include <limits>.

      carries on with status 2. This one is documented as a deviation, and it
      goes to the final review's fixes.
    - Medium: the "noexec" check is written out three times.
    - Low: 2 small findings.

    Ollama.

› Message from a11d2eb7fdf809086
  Re-checked develop-plan/tasks/hsh--control-flow.md against develop @ 2294d67.
  The plan's "What earlier tasks provide" list, interface assumptions
  (HshShell.h, HshAst.h, HshBuiltins.h, HshRedirection.h, HshSubshell.h,
  HshVariables.h) and test fixture (HshShellTest::Sh) all matched the real code

  SubshellScope/IsChildStage comments, etc. are all already in place as
  described).

  Added the three review findings as preliminary steps (with tests + acceptance
  checkboxes each), to be done before the control-flow work:
  1. IsChildStage doesn't see $(...) in a child stage's words → RunStage expands
  it in pass 1 before earlier in-shell stages run in pass 2 → deadlock (`: |
  /bin/echo $(cat)`). Fix: exclude command-substitution-bearing commands from
  IsChildStage. Test ChildStageCommandSubstitutionDoesNotDeadlock
  (HshPipelineTest.cpp, bounded std::async wait, no hang).
  2. SubshellScope restores m_jobs but not m_liveChildren, so a background job
  started inside a subshell leaks its processes forever. Fix: SubshellScope's
  destructor drops/waits the live children of jobs it is about to discard. Test
  SubshellBackgroundJobDoesNotLeakLiveChildren.
  3. RedirectionScope::Save dedups across the whole scope's lifetime, not per
  Apply call — latent only, since every current and planned call site uses
  exactly one Apply per scope. Marked optional/deferrable in the plan text. Test
  ASecondApplyOnOneScopeIsRefused (assert-based contract fix recommended).

  Also added: "no fixed sleeps" and "0 new HaisosOS.unittests tests" lesson
  notes, and an explicit "implement every named test" line, to the Tests
  section; updated the checked-against hash.

  Size: file grew 352 → 449 lines. Updated the "Size" line's code estimate to
  ~1150 changed lines / ~13 files, flagged as over the ~1100 rule of thumb, and
  recommended finding (3) (RedirectionScope) as the one to leave for the final
  whole-develop review if the implementer runs long — it's the only one of the

      610  
      611  ## The interactive protocol
      612  
      609 -(hsh--interactive implements it.) Keep a buffer of the lines t
          -yped since the                                                
      610 -last complete command; after each line, lex/parse the whole bu
          -ffer again from                                               
      611 -the start with `LexerOptions::interactive` set and `firstLine`
          - the number of                                                
      612 -that buffer's first line; on an error with `Incomplete()` show
          - PS2 and read                                                 
      613 -another line; otherwise run (or report) and empty the buffer. 
          -Re-reading the                                                
      614 -buffer each time keeps the lexer free of suspended state. With
          - `interactive`,                                               
      615 -two endings a script accepts become incomplete-input errors: a
          - heredoc whose                                                
      616 -delimiter line has not come yet, and a source ending in a back
          -slash-newline.                                                
      613 +`HshCommand::Run` makes the shell interactive when `-i` was gi
          +ven -- whatever                                               
      614 +the source (with `-c` it changes only `$-` and error handling,
          + as dash) -- or                                               
      615 +when the source is standard input and both descriptor 0 and de
          +scriptor 2                                                    
      616 +exist and are terminals (`RUN -i /bin/hsh` arranges that on th
          +e console).                                                   
      617 +                                                              
      618 +`Shell::RunInteractive` keeps a buffer of the lines typed sinc
          +e the last                                                    
      619 +complete command; after each line, the whole buffer is parsed 
          +again from the                                                
      620 +start with `ParserOptions::interactive` set (while the input h
          +as not ended)                                                 
      621 +and `firstLine` the number of that buffer's first line; on an 
          +error with                                                    
      622 +`incomplete` the buffer is kept, PS2 is shown and another line
          + is read;                                                     
      623 +otherwise the commands run (or the error is reported) and the 
          +buffer is                                                     
      624 +emptied. Every complete command of the buffer is collected bef
          +ore any runs:                                                 
      625 +`ParseNext` can return a Command whose heredoc is unterminated
          + and only                                                     
      626 +report it on the next call, and a line already run must not ru
          +n again when                                                  
      627 +the buffer is re-parsed a line later.                         
     … 23 more lines (ctrl+o to expand)

  The whole-develop review of develop @ 8eca554 is done: no critical findings
  and no sign of malice, but 5 high findings, so I wrote a fixes task. Counts: 0
  critical, 5 high, 8 medium, 4 low. Nothing ran on the host except dash and
  GNU wc, used to check disputed behaviour. Nothing is committed.

  - **Gate BLOCKs:** all 9 are protected-path changes from the user's own host
  commit 956b65e ("Minor update to develop-* skills"), and they are benign.
  `git_ssh.sh` only sets this clone's `core.sshCommand`, and only to an agent
  socket the user owns that is proven to reach origin. No task PR touched
  `.claude/`, `scripts/`, `.github/`, `notes/` or `HAISOS_VERSION`.
  - **Security:** hsh and every runtime still reach the OS only through
  `ICurrentProcess`. Build and test files only build and test, and no text in
  code, docs or tests is aimed at AI.

  **High (all go into the fixes task):**
  1. `cd` with a read-only PWD or OLDPWD is fatal. dash prints `cd: PWD: is read
  only`, returns 2 and goes on (from #38).
  2. A `>&` target such as `' 3'` or `+1` is accepted. dash requires exactly one
  digit and reports `Bad fd number` (from #36).
  3. Under `<<-`, tabs are also stripped from backslash-continued lines. dash
  keeps them and prints `a\tb` (HshLexer.cpp:917).
  4. wc prints nothing for an empty `--files0-from` list with `--total=always`
  or `--total=only`. GNU prints `0 0 0 total` or `0 0 0` (Wc.cpp:428).
  5. hsh's `--help` notes leave out several documented exceptions: PS1/PS2 not
  expanded, and `test`'s `-r -w -x -O -G -h -L -ef` limits. The root CLAUDE.md
  requires them there.

  **Medium:**
  - **New:** the in-shell pipe reads in quadratic time
  (HshUnboundedPipe.cpp:44). An endless in-shell stage piped into another
  in-shell stage never ends and its memory grows without bound, and this is not
  documented. Several comments and docs are stale, and some point at
  `develop-plan/goal.md` or at task ids, which `/develop-close` deletes.
  - **From the task reviews:** the read-only write test at
  FileDescriptorTest.cpp:162, the `ReleaseCountingDescriptor` copied 3 times,
  the two-lock `CreatePipe`, and the noexec check written out 3 times. All of
  these are in the fixes task.

  **Resolved already on develop** (no fix needed):
  - A background `$(...)` is no longer expanded in the foreground
  (HshShell.cpp:558): such a list now goes to a child hsh.
  - `case x in fi)` behaves as dash 0.5.12, which accepts `fi` as a pattern.
  - The `read` IFS merging, the `IsChildStage` redirection-target check,
  `"$@$@"` and the heredoc-body Incomplete flag are all fixed.

  **Two goal.md questions for the user** (goal.md not changed):
  - Scenario 3 runs `hello` against `hi*`, which dash answers `plain`; the tests
  run `/count.sh hi`.
  - The preamble's `/abc.txt` has no final newline as written, so scenarios 1
  and 2 would print `2` and `13 /out.txt`, not `3` and `14 /out.txt`. The haisos
  test adds an empty line before `END` to get the expected output.

      11 +                                                               
      12 +## Scenario 3: `hello` does not match `hi*`                    
      13 +                                                               
      14 +The scenario runs `RUN /bin/hsh /count.sh hello`, whose script 
         +ends with                                                      
      15 +`case "$1" in hi*) echo "greeted";; *) echo "plain";; esac`, an
         +d expects                                                      
      16 +`greeted`. `hi*` matches only words that start with `hi`, and `
         +hello` starts                                                  
      17 +with `he`, so bash, dash and hsh all print `plain`. Checked wit
         +h bash:                                                        
      18 +                                                               
      19 +```                                                            
      20 +$ bash -c 'case "$1" in hi*) echo greeted;; *) echo plain;; esa
         +c' _ hello                                                     
      21 +plain                                                          
      22 +$ bash -c 'case "$1" in hi*) echo greeted;; *) echo plain;; esa
         +c' _ hi                                                        
      23 +greeted                                                        
      24 +```                                                            
      25 +                                                               
      26 +The PR #39 review found this (hsh--control-flow,               
      27 +https://github.com/paul-neata/haisos/pull/39). The end-to-end t
         +est runs                                                       
      28 +`/count.sh hi` and expects `greeted`. To fix the scenario, run 
         +it with `hi`,                                                  
      29 +or keep `hello` and expect `plain`.
